# Algebra 1 Unit and Lesson Map

**Status:** Approved course architecture (2026-08-03)  
**Primary source:** enVision Florida B.E.S.T. Algebra 1, 2023  
**Savvas program ID:** 4204212b-5a54-35a0-97fb-d00633f5e83b

## Course Structure Summary

| Unit | Unit Title | Unit Purpose | Lesson Count | Unit Pretest | Unit Assessment |
|---|---|---|---:|---|---|
| Unit 1 | Algebra Readiness Foundations | Verify and strengthen prerequisite knowledge required for success throughout Algebra 1. | 7 | Required | Required |
| Unit 2 | Equations and Inequalities | Develop fluent equation and inequality reasoning that supports functions, systems, and modeling. | 7 | Required | Required |
| Unit 3 | Linear Equations and Linear/Absolute Value Functions | Connect linear and absolute-value representations, features, transformations, and models. | 10 | Required | Required |
| Unit 4 | Systems of Equations and Inequalities | Use graphical and algebraic methods to solve systems and interpret feasible solutions. | 6 | Required | Required |
| Unit 5 | Exponents, Radicals, Polynomials, and Factoring | Build algebraic structure and operations needed for exponential, polynomial, and quadratic work. | 12 | Required | Required |
| Unit 6 | Quadratic Functions and Equations | Analyze, model, and solve quadratic relationships using multiple representations and methods. | 11 | Required | Required |
| Unit 7 | Extended Function Families and Operations | Extend function reasoning to square-root, cubic, and cube-root families and operations. | 5 | Required | Required |
| Unit 8 | Data Representation and Analysis | Represent, analyze, and interpret numerical, categorical, and bivariate data. | 6 | Required | Required |

## Total Course Counts

| Item | Count |
|---|---:|
| Units | 8 |
| Lessons | 64 |
| Unit Pretests | 8 |
| Notebook Evidence Submissions | 64 |
| Checkpoints | 64 |
| Lesson or Cluster Quizzes | 21 |
| Unit Assessments | 8 |
| Projects/Labs/Performance Tasks | 8 unit synthesis/performance tasks |

## Governing Architecture Rules

- Every unit begins with an ungraded Unit Pretest.
- A pretest score of 90% or higher establishes eligibility for the separately administered accelerated pathway; it does not alter this course map.
- Every lesson begins with a combined Lesson Overview and Learning Objectives page and ends with Checkpoint and Help & TOR Support.
- Quiz placement follows verified Savvas assessment availability and may be lesson-level or cluster-based.
- Every unit ends with a cumulative Unit Synthesis lesson containing the graded Unit Assessment.
- Question counts, pool sizes, attempts, feedback timing, and reassessment settings must be finalized only after item-level Savvas inventory and approved MLA assessment policy review.
- COURSE_OVERVIEW.md, COURSE_CROSSWALK.md, and this file jointly govern Moodle production.

# Unit 1 — Algebra Readiness Foundations

## Unit Purpose

Verify and strengthen prerequisite knowledge required for success throughout Algebra 1.

## Foundational Role

Unit 1 addresses prerequisite number fluency, proportional reasoning, expressions, graph reading, mathematical habits, and diagnostic reflection. These recurring skills support every later unit. Readiness evidence guides support and eligibility for the separately administered accelerated pathway without changing the approved course sequence.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Explain the major concepts, representations, and vocabulary of algebra readiness foundations.
2. Apply accurate strategies and communicate complete mathematical reasoning.
3. Integrate the unit concepts in a cumulative synthesis and demonstrate mastery on the Unit 1 Assessment.

## Unit Standards

| MLA Standard | Role in Unit | External Alignment | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| MLA.A1.FND.4 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 1 lessons and assessments |

## Required Savvas Sources

| Source | Exact Savvas Location | Purpose | Student or Teacher Resource |
|---|---|---|---|
| enVision Florida B.E.S.T. Algebra 1, 2023 | Algebra 1 Readiness Test; Skills Review & Practice; Student Companion | Core instruction, practice, modeling, and assessment evidence | Student and Teacher |
| Savvas assessment resources | Unit 1 aligned lesson, topic, readiness, review, and assessment banks | Pretest, quizzes, and unit-assessment source inventory | Teacher-protected |

## Unit Moodle Sequence

1. Unit 1 Overview
2. Unit 1 Pretest
3. Lesson 1.1 — Diagnostic Reflection and Mathematical Habits
4. Lesson 1.2 — Rational-Number Fluency
5. Lesson 1.3 — Fractions, Decimals, and Percents
6. Lesson 1.4 — Ratios, Rates, and Proportional Reasoning
7. Lesson 1.5 — Evaluating and Simplifying Expressions
8. Lesson 1.6 — Coordinate Plane and Graph Reading
9. Lesson 1.7 — Foundations Synthesis and Unit Assessment
10. Unit Completion and Next-Step Instructions

# Unit 1, Lesson 1 — Diagnostic Reflection and Mathematical Habits

## Lesson Identification

| Field | Requirement |
|---|---|
| MLA Lesson ID | ALG1-U1-L1 |
| Savvas Source | enVision Florida B.E.S.T. Algebra 1, 2023 — Algebra 1 Readiness Test; Student Companion |
| Estimated Scope | One standard instructional lesson |
| Prerequisite Knowledge | Prior lessons in Unit 1; prerequisite skills identified by the Unit 1 Pretest |
| Leads To | The next lesson and Unit 1 synthesis |

## Lesson Purpose

Students explain and apply diagnostic reflection and mathematical habits at this point in the sequence so they can produce diagnostic reflection and progress toward unit mastery.

## Learning Objectives

By the end of the lesson, students will be able to:

1. Explain the central concepts and vocabulary of diagnostic reflection and mathematical habits.
2. Apply an appropriate strategy accurately and show organized mathematical reasoning.
3. Interpret or verify the result and connect it to the lesson context.

## Standards

| Standard | Instructional Role | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| MLA.A1.FND.4 | Introduced, practiced, reinforced, or assessed as specified in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Notebook evidence; checkpoint; |

## Lesson Evidence Summary

### Notebook Evidence

Students must include:

1. Labeled notes and vocabulary for Diagnostic Reflection and Mathematical Habits.
2. At least one complete worked example with all reasoning shown.
3. A written interpretation, verification, or error analysis.

### Checkpoint Evidence

Students must submit:

1. Diagnostic reflection.
2. A concise explanation of the strategy used.
3. Work labeled with ALG1-U1-L1.

### Other Required Evidence

- Complete all assigned Savvas practice and Moodle activities.

## Lesson Moodle Page Sequence

1. Lesson Overview and Learning Objectives
2. Concept Instruction and Savvas Resource
3. Guided Practice and Notebook Task
4. Independent Application
5. Notebook Evidence Submission
6. Checkpoint Submission
7. Help & TOR Support

Notebook Evidence Submission — U1 L1

### Moodle Activity Type

Assignment

### Required Evidence

2. One complete worked example.
3. Written interpretation, verification, or error analysis.

### Student Submission Instructions

Submit one readable PDF or up to three JPG/PNG files. Handwritten work is permitted when clearly scanned or photographed. Every page must include the student name and ALG1-U1-L1.

### Rubric Requirements

Create criteria for completeness, mathematical accuracy, reasoning, notation/labels, and standard alignment. Each criterion must define full, partial, and no-credit performance, expected evidence, acceptable alternatives, common errors, and the relevant standard.

### Answer-Key Requirements

The protected TOR guide must provide correct answers, worked solutions, explanations, acceptable alternatives, units, labels, expected graphs or diagrams, and supporting evidence.

### TOR Grading Requirement

The rubric and protected guide must allow qualified TORs to score the work consistently without reconstructing answers.

### Moodle Activity Title

Checkpoint Submission — U1 L1

### Moodle Activity Type

Assignment or Quiz, selected to preserve the approved evidence format

### Checkpoint Purpose

Demonstrate independent progress toward the lesson objectives through diagnostic reflection.

### Required Student Product

- Diagnostic reflection
- Supporting mathematical work
- Written interpretation or justification

### Required Student Actions

1. Complete the checkpoint independently.
2. Verify calculations, labels, and representations.
3. Submit the required evidence in Moodle.

### Required Submission

Submit one PDF or up to three image files, labeled with student name and ALG1-U1-L1. Complete any required Savvas submission and the Moodle submission.

### Checkpoint Rubric Requirements

Include the required component, expected response, acceptable variation, reasoning, scoring levels, measured standards, misconception, TOR feedback, and remediation.

### Checkpoint Answer-Key Requirements

Include protected correct answers, worked solutions, calculations, units, labels, expected representations, a proficient response, alternatives, and mastery evidence.

## Lesson Completion Standard

The lesson is complete when the student has viewed all pages, completed assigned Savvas resources and Notebook Tasks, submitted Notebook Evidence and the Checkpoint, completed all required practice, met completion conditions, and addressed TOR feedback or revisions.

# Unit 1, Lesson 4 — Ratios, Rates, and Proportional Reasoning

| Field | Requirement |

## Lesson Purpose

## Learning Objectives

1. Explain the central concepts and vocabulary of ratios, rates, and proportional reasoning.

## Standards

# Unit 2 — Equations and Inequalities

## Unit Purpose

Develop fluent equation and inequality reasoning that supports functions, systems, and modeling.

## Relationship to Prior and Future Units

This unit requires the skills and representations established in prior units, extends them through equations and inequalities, and prepares students for linear equations and linear/absolute value functions.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Explain the major concepts, representations, and vocabulary of equations and inequalities.
2. Apply accurate strategies and communicate complete mathematical reasoning.
3. Integrate the unit concepts in a cumulative synthesis and demonstrate mastery on the Unit 2 Assessment.

## Unit Standards

| MLA Standard | Role in Unit | External Alignment | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| MLA.A1.AR.1 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 2 lessons and assessments |

# Unit 2, Lesson 1 — Solving Linear Equations

## Lesson Identification

| Field | Requirement |
|---|---|
| MLA Lesson ID | ALG1-U2-L1 |
| Savvas Source | enVision Florida B.E.S.T. Algebra 1, 2023 — Savvas 1-1 |
| Estimated Scope | One standard instructional lesson |
| Prerequisite Knowledge | Prior lessons in Unit 2; prerequisite skills identified by the Unit 2 Pretest |
| Leads To | The next lesson and Unit 2 synthesis |

## Lesson Purpose

Students solve and justify solving linear equations at this point in the sequence so they can produce equation-solving notebook evidence and progress toward unit mastery.

## Learning Objectives

By the end of the lesson, students will be able to:

1. Explain the central concepts and vocabulary of solving linear equations.
2. Apply an appropriate strategy accurately and show organized mathematical reasoning.
3. Interpret or verify the result and connect it to the lesson context.

## Standards

| Standard | Instructional Role | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| MLA.A1.AR.1, MLA.A1.AR.2, MLA.A1.AR.8, MLA.A1.FND.5 | Introduced, practiced, reinforced, or assessed as specified in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Notebook evidence; checkpoint; |

## Lesson Evidence Summary

### Notebook Evidence

Students must include:

1. Labeled notes and vocabulary for Solving Linear Equations.
2. At least one complete worked example with all reasoning shown.
3. A written interpretation, verification, or error analysis.

### Checkpoint Evidence

Students must submit:

1. Equation-solving notebook evidence.
2. A concise explanation of the strategy used.
3. Work labeled with ALG1-U2-L1.

### Other Required Evidence

- Complete all assigned Savvas practice and Moodle activities.

## Lesson Moodle Page Sequence

1. Lesson Overview and Learning Objectives
2. Concept Instruction and Savvas Resource
3. Guided Practice and Notebook Task
4. Independent Application
5. Notebook Evidence Submission
6. Checkpoint Submission
7. Help & TOR Support

# Unit 3 — Linear Equations and Linear/Absolute Value Functions

## Unit Purpose

Connect linear and absolute-value representations, features, transformations, and models.

## Relationship to Prior and Future Units

This unit requires the skills and representations established in prior units, extends them through linear equations and linear/absolute value functions, and prepares students for systems of equations and inequalities.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Explain the major concepts, representations, and vocabulary of linear equations and linear/absolute value functions.
2. Apply accurate strategies and communicate complete mathematical reasoning.
3. Integrate the unit concepts in a cumulative synthesis and demonstrate mastery on the Unit 3 Assessment.

## Unit Standards

|---|---|---|---|

# Unit 3, Lesson 1 — Slope-Intercept Form

## Lesson Identification

| Field | Requirement |
|---|---|
| MLA Lesson ID | ALG1-U3-L1 |
| Savvas Source | enVision Florida B.E.S.T. Algebra 1, 2023 — Savvas 2-1 |
| Estimated Scope | One standard instructional lesson |
| Prerequisite Knowledge | Prior lessons in Unit 3; prerequisite skills identified by the Unit 3 Pretest |
| Leads To | The next lesson and Unit 3 synthesis |

## Lesson Purpose

Students explain and apply slope-intercept form at this point in the sequence so they can produce equation, graph, and context translation and progress toward unit mastery.

## Learning Objectives

By the end of the lesson, students will be able to:

1. Explain the central concepts and vocabulary of slope-intercept form.
2. Apply an appropriate strategy accurately and show organized mathematical reasoning.
3. Interpret or verify the result and connect it to the lesson context.

## Standards

| Standard | Instructional Role | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| MLA.A1.LR.1, MLA.A1.LR.2, MLA.A1.LR.3, MLA.A1.LR.4, MLA.A1.LR.5, MLA.A1.FN.1, MLA.A1.FN.2, MLA.A1.FN.3, MLA.A1.FN.4 | Introduced, practiced, reinforced, or assessed as specified in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Notebook evidence; checkpoint; |

## Lesson Evidence Summary

### Notebook Evidence

Students must include:

1. Labeled notes and vocabulary for Slope-Intercept Form.
2. At least one complete worked example with all reasoning shown.
3. A written interpretation, verification, or error analysis.

### Checkpoint Evidence

Students must submit:

1. Equation, graph, and context translation.
2. A concise explanation of the strategy used.
3. Work labeled with ALG1-U3-L1.

### Other Required Evidence

- Complete all assigned Savvas practice and Moodle activities.

# Unit 4 — Systems of Equations and Inequalities

## Unit Purpose

Use graphical and algebraic methods to solve systems and interpret feasible solutions.

## Relationship to Prior and Future Units

This unit requires the skills and representations established in prior units, extends them through systems of equations and inequalities, and prepares students for exponents, radicals, polynomials, and factoring.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Explain the major concepts, representations, and vocabulary of systems of equations and inequalities.
2. Apply accurate strategies and communicate complete mathematical reasoning.
3. Integrate the unit concepts in a cumulative synthesis and demonstrate mastery on the Unit 4 Assessment.

## Unit Standards

| MLA Standard | Role in Unit | External Alignment | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| MLA.A1.AR.3 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 4 lessons and assessments |
| MLA.A1.AR.4 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 4 lessons and assessments |
| MLA.A1.AR.5 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 4 lessons and assessments |
| MLA.A1.AR.6 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 4 lessons and assessments |
| MLA.A1.AR.7 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 4 lessons and assessments |

# Unit 5 — Exponents, Radicals, Polynomials, and Factoring

## Unit Purpose

Build algebraic structure and operations needed for exponential, polynomial, and quadratic work.

## Relationship to Prior and Future Units

This unit requires the skills and representations established in prior units, extends them through exponents, radicals, polynomials, and factoring, and prepares students for quadratic functions and equations.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Explain the major concepts, representations, and vocabulary of exponents, radicals, polynomials, and factoring.
2. Apply accurate strategies and communicate complete mathematical reasoning.
3. Integrate the unit concepts in a cumulative synthesis and demonstrate mastery on the Unit 5 Assessment.

## Unit Standards

# Unit 6 — Quadratic Functions and Equations

## Unit Purpose

Analyze, model, and solve quadratic relationships using multiple representations and methods.

## Relationship to Prior and Future Units

This unit requires the skills and representations established in prior units, extends them through quadratic functions and equations, and prepares students for extended function families and operations.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Explain the major concepts, representations, and vocabulary of quadratic functions and equations.
2. Apply accurate strategies and communicate complete mathematical reasoning.
3. Integrate the unit concepts in a cumulative synthesis and demonstrate mastery on the Unit 6 Assessment.

## Unit Standards

| MLA Standard | Role in Unit | External Alignment | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|

| MLA.A1.QE.3 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 6 lessons and assessments |

| MLA.A1.QE.5 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 6 lessons and assessments |

# Unit 6, Lesson 1 — Key Features of a Quadratic Function

## Lesson Identification

| Field | Requirement |
|---|---|
| MLA Lesson ID | ALG1-U6-L1 |
| Savvas Source | enVision Florida B.E.S.T. Algebra 1, 2023 — Savvas 7-1 |
| Estimated Scope | One standard instructional lesson |
| Prerequisite Knowledge | Prior lessons in Unit 6; prerequisite skills identified by the Unit 6 Pretest |
| Leads To | The next lesson and Unit 6 synthesis |

## Lesson Purpose

Students explain and apply key features of a quadratic function at this point in the sequence so they can produce feature identification and progress toward unit mastery.

## Learning Objectives

By the end of the lesson, students will be able to:

1. Explain the central concepts and vocabulary of key features of a quadratic function.
2. Apply an appropriate strategy accurately and show organized mathematical reasoning.
3. Interpret or verify the result and connect it to the lesson context.

## Standards

| Standard | Instructional Role | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| MLA.A1.QE.1, MLA.A1.QE.2, MLA.A1.QE.3, MLA.A1.QE.4, MLA.A1.QE.5, MLA.A1.QE.6, MLA.A1.FN.5, MLA.A1.FN.6, MLA.A1.FN.7 | Introduced, practiced, reinforced, or assessed as specified in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Notebook evidence; checkpoint; |

1. Labeled notes and vocabulary for Key Features of a Quadratic Function.

3. A written interpretation, verification, or error analysis.

### Checkpoint Evidence

Students must submit:

1. Feature identification.
2. A concise explanation of the strategy used.
3. Work labeled with ALG1-U6-L1.

# Unit 7 — Extended Function Families and Operations

## Unit Purpose

Extend function reasoning to square-root, cubic, and cube-root families and operations.

## Relationship to Prior and Future Units

This unit requires the skills and representations established in prior units, extends them through extended function families and operations, and prepares students for data representation and analysis.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Explain the major concepts, representations, and vocabulary of extended function families and operations.
2. Apply accurate strategies and communicate complete mathematical reasoning.
3. Integrate the unit concepts in a cumulative synthesis and demonstrate mastery on the Unit 7 Assessment.

## Unit Standards

| MLA Standard | Role in Unit | External Alignment | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| MLA.A1.FN.1 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 7 lessons and assessments |
| MLA.A1.FN.2 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 7 lessons and assessments |

## Required Savvas Sources

| Source | Exact Savvas Location | Purpose | Student or Teacher Resource |
|---|---|---|---|
| enVision Florida B.E.S.T. Algebra 1, 2023 | Topic 9: Working With Functions | Core instruction, practice, modeling, and assessment evidence | Student and Teacher |
| Savvas assessment resources | Unit 7 aligned lesson, topic, readiness, review, and assessment banks | Pretest, quizzes, and unit-assessment source inventory | Teacher-protected |

## Unit Moodle Sequence

1. Unit 7 Overview
2. Unit 7 Pretest
3. Lesson 7.1 — Square Root Functions
4. Lesson 7.2 — Cubic and Cube Root Functions
5. Lesson 7.3 — Analyzing Functions
6. Lesson 7.4 — Operations With Functions
7. Lesson 7.5 — Extended Functions Synthesis and Unit Assessment
8. Unit Completion and Next-Step Instructions

## Notebook Evidence Submission Specification

### Moodle Activity Title

Notebook Evidence Submission — U7 L4

### Moodle Activity Type

Assignment

### Required Evidence

1. Labeled notes and vocabulary.
2. One complete worked example.
3. Written interpretation, verification, or error analysis.

### Student Submission Instructions

Submit one readable PDF or up to three JPG/PNG files. Handwritten work is permitted when clearly scanned or photographed. Every page must include the student name and ALG1-U7-L4.

### Rubric Requirements

Create criteria for completeness, mathematical accuracy, reasoning, notation/labels, and standard alignment. Each criterion must define full, partial, and no-credit performance, expected evidence, acceptable alternatives, common errors, and the relevant standard.

### Answer-Key Requirements

The protected TOR guide must provide correct answers, worked solutions, explanations, acceptable alternatives, units, labels, expected graphs or diagrams, and supporting evidence.

### TOR Grading Requirement

The rubric and protected guide must allow qualified TORs to score the work consistently without reconstructing answers.

- Supporting mathematical work
- Written interpretation or justification

1. Complete the checkpoint independently.
2. Verify calculations, labels, and representations.
3. Submit the required evidence in Moodle.

Include protected correct answers, worked solutions, calculations, units, labels, expected representations, a proficient response, alternatives, and mastery evidence.

### Assessment Type

Lesson or Cluster Quiz

### Placement

After Lesson 7.4

### Source

Savvas 9-4 and the corresponding Savvas assessment bank

### Question-Pool Requirement

- Pool size: Use the complete verified aligned Savvas pool.
- Questions delivered: Set after source inventory confirms coverage and dependencies.
- Randomization: Yes, only when stimulus-question relationships remain intact.
- Attempts: Follow approved MLA assessment policy.
- Feedback: Provide feedback after the attempt according to approved policy.
- Correct answers shown: Protect answers until the approved release point.
- Mastery or completion requirement: Apply approved course mastery settings.
- Standards assessed: MLA.A1.FN.1, MLA.A1.FN.2

# Unit 8 — Data Representation and Analysis

## Unit Purpose

Represent, analyze, and interpret numerical, categorical, and bivariate data.

## Relationship to Prior and Future Units

This unit requires the skills and representations established in prior units, extends them through data representation and analysis, and prepares students for course synthesis and EOC preparation.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Explain the major concepts, representations, and vocabulary of data representation and analysis.
2. Apply accurate strategies and communicate complete mathematical reasoning.
3. Integrate the unit concepts in a cumulative synthesis and demonstrate mastery on the Unit 8 Assessment.

## Unit Standards

| MLA Standard | Role in Unit | External Alignment | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| MLA.A1.DF.3 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 8 lessons and assessments |
| MLA.A1.DF.4 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 8 lessons and assessments |
| MLA.A1.DF.5 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 8 lessons and assessments |
| MLA.A1.DF.6 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 8 lessons and assessments |
| MLA.A1.DF.7 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 8 lessons and assessments |
| MLA.A1.DF.8 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 8 lessons and assessments |
| MLA.A1.DF.9 | See COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Florida B.E.S.T., CPALMS, Common Core, SAT, and ACT alignments in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Unit 8 lessons and assessments |

## Required Savvas Sources

| Source | Exact Savvas Location | Purpose | Student or Teacher Resource |
|---|---|---|---|
| enVision Florida B.E.S.T. Algebra 1, 2023 | Topic 10: Analyzing Data | Core instruction, practice, modeling, and assessment evidence | Student and Teacher |
| Savvas assessment resources | Unit 8 aligned lesson, topic, readiness, review, and assessment banks | Pretest, quizzes, and unit-assessment source inventory | Teacher-protected |

## Unit Moodle Sequence

2. Unit 8 Pretest

4. Lesson 8.2 — Representing Categorical Data

1. Labeled notes and vocabulary for Data Synthesis and Unit Assessment.
2. At least one complete worked example with all reasoning shown.
3. A written interpretation, verification, or error analysis.

### Checkpoint Evidence

Students must submit:

1. Integrated investigation and Unit 8 Assessment.
2. A concise explanation of the strategy used.
3. Work labeled with ALG1-U8-L6.

### Other Required Evidence

- Complete the Unit 8 synthesis and graded assessment.

## Lesson Moodle Page Sequence

1. Lesson Overview and Learning Objectives
2. Concept Instruction and Savvas Resource
3. Guided Practice and Notebook Task
4. Independent Application
5. Notebook Evidence Submission
6. Checkpoint Submission
7. Help & TOR Support

## Page 1 — Lesson Overview and Learning Objectives

### Moodle Activity Type

Page

### Student-Facing Page Title

ALGEBRA 1 | UNIT 8 | LESSON 6

Data Synthesis and Unit Assessment

### Required Content

Include the course, unit and lesson identifiers; ALG1-U8-L6; lesson focus; the three measurable objectives above; standards; evidence checklist; completion expectations; and TOR support statement.

### Evidence Checklist

- Notebook Evidence: labeled notes, worked example, and interpretation.
- Checkpoint Evidence: Integrated investigation and Unit 8 Assessment and explanation.
- Unit 8 synthesis and assessment evidence.

### Visual Requirements

Use the approved Algebra 1 header, objective box, evidence box, TOR Support box, course colors, accessible icons, and meaningful alternative text.

### Student Completion Standard

The student can state what the lesson teaches, what must be completed and submitted, and where help is available.

## Page 2 — Concept Instruction and Savvas Resource

### Instructional Role

Concept Instruction / Savvas eText

### Moodle Activity Type

Page and URL

### Savvas Resource

| Field | Requirement |
|---|---|
| Exact resource title | Data Synthesis and Unit Assessment |
| Savvas location | Topic 10 review, modeling, performance, and assessment resources |
| Link method | Approved direct link when stable; otherwise exact Savvas navigation route |
| Student access | Student Savvas account |
| Begin at | Opening screen or heading for Topic 10 review, modeling, performance, and assessment resources |
| Complete | Assigned instruction, examples, and practice aligned to the lesson objectives |
| Stop after | Final assigned problem or screen identified during source inventory |

### Student Instructional Focus

Students attend to vocabulary, representations, procedures, reasoning, and evidence needed to produce integrated investigation and unit 8 assessment.

### Required Student Actions

1. Open the exact Savvas resource.
2. Begin at the identified lesson opening.
3. Complete the assigned instruction and examples.
4. Record vocabulary and one worked example.
5. Complete the assigned prompts.
6. Stop at the specified endpoint.
7. Return to Moodle and continue to guided practice.

### Notebook Requirements

Use complete mathematical notation; show calculations and reasoning; include required graphs, tables, or diagrams; label the page ALG1-U8-L6; and submit the evidence through Moodle.

### Evidence Requirement

Notebook work and the checkpoint are submitted in Moodle. Savvas submission is also required only when the assigned resource records student work there.

### Visual Requirements

Use the approved header and functional boxes, accessible diagrams and tables, MathJax for equations, descriptive link labels, and alternative text.

### TOR Support Requirement

Tell students to contact the TOR if the link, resource, or activity does not open, load, or remain clear.

## Page 3 — Guided Practice and Notebook Task

### Instructional Role

Guided Practice / Notebook Task

### Moodle Activity Type

Page

### Required Student Actions

Complete scaffolded examples, record the required notebook evidence, check each result, and proceed only after addressing errors.

### Evidence Requirement

The notebook must contain the three ordered evidence items listed above and be readable, complete, and labeled ALG1-U8-L6.

## Page 4 — Independent Application

### Instructional Role

Independent Application

### Moodle Activity Type

Page or Assignment

### Required Student Actions

Apply the lesson strategy independently, show all required reasoning, interpret the result, and prepare the work for submission.

## Notebook Evidence Submission Specification

### Moodle Activity Title

Notebook Evidence Submission — U8 L6

### Moodle Activity Type

Assignment

### Required Evidence

1. Labeled notes and vocabulary.
2. One complete worked example.
3. Written interpretation, verification, or error analysis.

### Student Submission Instructions

Submit one readable PDF or up to three JPG/PNG files. Handwritten work is permitted when clearly scanned or photographed. Every page must include the student name and ALG1-U8-L6.

### Rubric Requirements

Create criteria for completeness, mathematical accuracy, reasoning, notation/labels, and standard alignment. Each criterion must define full, partial, and no-credit performance, expected evidence, acceptable alternatives, common errors, and the relevant standard.

### Answer-Key Requirements

The protected TOR guide must provide correct answers, worked solutions, explanations, acceptable alternatives, units, labels, expected graphs or diagrams, and supporting evidence.

### TOR Grading Requirement

The rubric and protected guide must allow qualified TORs to score the work consistently without reconstructing answers.

## Checkpoint Specification

### Moodle Activity Title

Checkpoint Submission — U8 L6

### Moodle Activity Type

Assignment or Quiz, selected to preserve the approved evidence format

### Checkpoint Purpose

Demonstrate independent progress toward the lesson objectives through integrated investigation and unit 8 assessment.

### Required Student Product

- Integrated investigation and Unit 8 Assessment
- Supporting mathematical work
- Written interpretation or justification

### Required Student Actions

1. Complete the checkpoint independently.
2. Verify calculations, labels, and representations.
3. Submit the required evidence in Moodle.

### Required Submission

Submit one PDF or up to three image files, labeled with student name and ALG1-U8-L6. Complete any required Savvas submission and the Moodle submission.

### Checkpoint Rubric Requirements

Include the required component, expected response, acceptable variation, reasoning, scoring levels, measured standards, misconception, TOR feedback, and remediation.

### Checkpoint Answer-Key Requirements

Include protected correct answers, worked solutions, calculations, units, labels, expected representations, a proficient response, alternatives, and mastery evidence.

## Lesson Completion Standard

The lesson is complete when the student has viewed all pages, completed assigned Savvas resources and Notebook Tasks, submitted Notebook Evidence and the Checkpoint, completed all required practice, met completion conditions, and addressed TOR feedback or revisions.

## Lesson Production Notes

Verify accessibility, stable Savvas access, MathJax rendering, table and graph readability, media and licensing requirements, teacher-only answer protection, mobile usability, and completion tracking.

---


# Final Production Authority

This map defines the approved unit sequence, lesson sequence, required evidence, Moodle activity structure, and assessment placement for Algebra 1. The Moodle Course Builder must implement it with the assigned Savvas materials and may not independently redesign the academic architecture.
